(51b)

We observe that the loop-diagrams are identical in
Eqs. (
51a
)
and (
51b
), further qualified below. Using
Eqs. (
103
)
-
Eq. (
101
)
, the cross-species quartic vertices to one loop are

\displaystyle\left.\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{1}\phi_{1}\phi_{2}\phi_{2}}^{abcd}(\mathbf{k})\right|_{NP}=-\mathbf{k}^{2}\delta^{ab}\delta^{cd}g_{12}\left[1-\left(u_{1}\frac{n+2}{6}\frac{\Gamma_{1}}{D_{1}^{2}}+u_{2}\frac{n+2}{6}\frac{\Gamma_{2}}{D_{2}^{2}}+\frac{2g_{12}\Gamma_{2}}{D_{2}(D_{1}+D_{2})}+\frac{2g_{21}\Gamma_{1}}{D_{1}(D_{1}+D_{2})}\right)\frac{\Gamma\left(\epsilon/2\right)}{(4\pi)^{d/2}}\mu^{-\epsilon}\right]

(52a)

\displaystyle\left.\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{2}\phi_{2}\phi_{1}\phi_{1}}^{abcd}(\mathbf{k})\right|_{NP}=-\mathbf{k}^{2}\delta^{ab}\delta^{cd}g_{21}\left[1-\left(u_{1}\frac{n+2}{6}\frac{\Gamma_{1}}{D_{1}^{2}}+u_{2}\frac{n+2}{6}\frac{\Gamma_{2}}{D_{2}^{2}}+\frac{2g_{12}\Gamma_{2}}{D_{2}(D_{1}+D_{2})}+\frac{2g_{21}\Gamma_{1}}{D_{1}(D_{1}+D_{2})}\right)\frac{\Gamma\left(\epsilon/2\right)}{(4\pi)^{d/2}}\mu^{-\epsilon}\right]

(52b)

with identical expressions in the square brackets, so that both vertices are being renormalised identically.
Eqs. (
52
)

allow us to read off
\mathcal{I}_{g_{ij}}
as of
Eqs. (
35d
)
and (
38f
)

\mathcal{I}_{g_{12}}=\mathcal{I}_{g_{21}}=Z_{\Gamma_{2}}Z_{g_{12}}=Z_{\Gamma_{1}}Z_{g_{21}}=1-\left(u_{1}\frac{n+2}{6}\frac{\Gamma_{1}}{D_{1}^{2}}+u_{2}\frac{n+2}{6}\frac{\Gamma_{2}}{D_{2}^{2}}+\frac{2g_{12}\Gamma_{2}}{D_{2}(D_{1}+D_{2})}+\frac{2g_{21}\Gamma_{1}}{D_{1}(D_{1}+D_{2})}\right)\frac{\Gamma\left(\epsilon/2\right)}{(4\pi)^{d/2}}\mu^{-\epsilon}

(53)

using that
Z_{\bm{\tilde{\phi}}_{i}}Z_{\bm{\phi}_{i}}Z^{2}_{\bm{\phi}_{j}}=Z^{-2}_{\bm{\tilde{\phi}}_{j}}=Z_{\Gamma_{j}}
as
Z_{\bm{\tilde{\phi}}_{i}}Z_{\bm{\phi}_{i}}=1
,
Eq. (
41
)
, and
Z_{\bm{\tilde{\phi}}_{i}}^{2}=1/Z_{\Gamma_{i}}
,
Eq. (
43
)
.

All
Z
-factors,
Eqs. (
47
)
, (
50
) and (
53
) are expressed in terms of ten bare parameters of the theory, namely
\tau_{i}
,
u_{i}
,
D_{i}
,
\Gamma_{i}
,
g_{12}
and
g_{21}
. However, we can define new dimensionless expansion parameters to reduce their number to seven,

\tilde{u}_{i}=\frac{u_{i}\Gamma_{i}}{(4\pi)^{d/2}D_{i}^{2}},\penalty\ w=\frac{D_{1}}{D_{2}},\penalty\ \tilde{g}_{12}=\frac{g_{12}\Gamma_{2}}{(4\pi)^{d/2}D_{1}D_{2}},\penalty\ \tilde{g}_{21}=\frac{g_{21}\Gamma_{1}}{(4\pi)^{d/2}D_{1}D_{2}}.

(54)

We further define their renormalised counterparts as

\tilde{u}_{i,R}=Z_{\tilde{u}_{i}}\tilde{u}_{i}\mu^{-\epsilon},\penalty\ w_{R}=Z_{w}w,\penalty\ \tilde{g}_{12,R}=Z_{\tilde{g}_{12}}\tilde{g}_{12}\mu^{-\epsilon},\penalty\ \tilde{g}_{21,R}=Z_{\tilde{g}_{21}}\tilde{g}_{21}\mu^{-\epsilon}

(55)

With these definitions in place, we derive their
Z
factors in terms of
\{\tilde{u}_{i,R},\tilde{g}_{12,R},\tilde{g}_{21,R},w_{R}\}
to leading order in
1/\epsilon
and
\lambda_{R}
. Using
Eqs. (
54
)
and (
55
) as well as
\Gamma(\epsilon/2)\sim 2/\epsilon
for small
\epsilon
, we have together with
Eq. (
47a
)
and (
47b
)

\displaystyle Z_{\tau_{1}}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{n+2}{3}\frac{\tilde{u}_{1,R}}{\epsilon}-n\frac{\tilde{g}_{12,R}w_{R}}{\epsilon}\frac{\tau_{2,R}}{\tau_{1,R}}+\mathcal{O}(\lambda_{R}^{2})

(56a)

\displaystyle Z_{\tau_{2}}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{n+2}{3}\frac{\tilde{u}_{2,R}}{\epsilon}-n\frac{\tilde{g}_{21,R}}{\epsilon w_{R}}\frac{\tau_{1,R}}{\tau_{2,R}}+\mathcal{O}(\lambda_{R}^{2})\ ,

(56b)

together with
Eqs. (
47c
)
and (
47d
)

\displaystyle Z_{w}

\displaystyle=1+\mathcal{O}(\lambda_{R}^{2})\ ,

(56c)

together with
Eqs. (
50a
)
, (
50b
), (
47c
) and (
47d
)

\displaystyle Z_{\tilde{u}_{1}}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{n+8}{3}\frac{\tilde{u}_{1,R}}{\epsilon}-3n\frac{\tilde{g}_{12,R}\tilde{g}_{21,R}}{\tilde{u}_{1,R}\epsilon}+\mathcal{O}(\lambda_{R}^{2})

(56d)

\displaystyle Z_{\tilde{u}_{2}}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{n+8}{3}\frac{\tilde{u}_{2,R}}{\epsilon}-3n\frac{\tilde{g}_{12,R}\tilde{g}_{21,R}}{\tilde{u}_{2,R}\epsilon}+\mathcal{O}(\lambda_{R}^{2})\ ,

(56e)

and finally with
Eqs. (
53
)
, (
47c
) and (
47d
)

\displaystyle Z_{\tilde{g}_{12}}

\displaystyle=Z_{\tilde{g}_{21}}=1-\frac{n+2}{3}\frac{\tilde{u}_{1,R}}{\epsilon}-\frac{n+2}{3}\frac{\tilde{u}_{2,R}}{\epsilon}-4\frac{w_{R}}{1+w_{R}}\frac{\tilde{g}_{12,R}}{\epsilon}-\frac{4}{1+w_{R}}\frac{\tilde{g}_{21,R}}{\epsilon}+\mathcal{O}(\lambda_{R}^{2})\ .

(56f)

where we used
Z_{D_{1}}=Z_{D_{2}}=1+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{2})
,
Eqs. (
47c
)
and (
47d
), to obtain the
Z-
factors for
\tilde{u}_{i}
,
Z_{g_{12}}
and
Z_{g_{21}}
. To leading order in
\lambda_{R}
we simply replaced the bare couplings by their renormalised counterparts using
Eq. (
55
)
. In the next section, we use these
Z
-factors
Eqs. (
56
)
to characterise the fixed points of the theory.

III.4
Fixed Points of the RG flow

Eqs. (
56
)
show all
Z
-factors in terms of the new effective expansion parameters

\tilde{u}_{i,R}
,
\tilde{g}_{12,R}
,
\tilde{g}_{21,R}
and
w_{R}
.

To identify the fixed points of our theory, we first calculate the
\beta
-functions for these couplings using their definitions
Eqs. (
55
)
:

\displaystyle\beta_{\tilde{u}_{i}}

\displaystyle=\mu\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{u}_{i,R}}{\mathrm{d}\mu}=\tilde{u}_{i,R}\left(-\epsilon+\mu\frac{\mathrm{d}\ln Z_{\tilde{u}_{i}}}{\mathrm{d}\mu}\right)\ ,\qquad

\displaystyle\beta_{w}

\displaystyle=\mu\frac{\mathrm{d}w_{R}}{\mathrm{d}\mu}=w_{R}\left(\mu\frac{\mathrm{d}\log Z_{w}}{\mathrm{d}\mu}\right)

(57a)

\displaystyle\beta_{\tilde{g}_{12}}

\displaystyle=\mu\frac{\partial\tilde{g}_{12,R}}{\partial\mu}=\tilde{g}_{12,R}\left(-\epsilon+\mu\frac{\mathrm{d}\ln Z_{\tilde{g}_{12}}}{\mathrm{d}\mu}\right)\ ,\qquad

\displaystyle\beta_{\tilde{g}_{21}}

\displaystyle=\mu\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{g}_{21,R}}{\mathrm{d}\mu}=\tilde{g}_{21,R}\left(-\epsilon+\mu\frac{\mathrm{d}\ln Z_{\tilde{g}_{21}}}{\mathrm{d}\mu}\right)\ .

(57b)

To determine them explicitly, we use
Eqs. (
56
)
, replacing
\mu\mathrm{d}\lambda_{R}/\mathrm{d}\mu
by their leading orders
-\epsilon\lambda_{R}
according to
Eqs. (
57
)
,

\displaystyle\beta_{\tilde{u}_{1}}

\displaystyle=-\epsilon\tilde{u}_{1,R}+\frac{n+8}{3}\tilde{u}_{1,R}^{2}+3n\tilde{g}_{12,R}\tilde{g}_{21,R}

(58a)

\displaystyle\beta_{\tilde{u}_{2}}

\displaystyle=-\epsilon\tilde{u}_{2,R}+\frac{n+8}{3}\tilde{u}_{2,R}^{2}+3n\tilde{g}_{12,R}\tilde{g}_{21,R}

(58b)

\displaystyle\beta_{\tilde{g}_{12}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{g}_{12,R}\left(-\epsilon+4\frac{w\tilde{g}_{12,R}+\tilde{g}_{21,R}}{1+w}+\frac{n+2}{3}\tilde{u}_{1,R}+\frac{n+2}{3}\tilde{u}_{2,R}\right)

(58c)

\displaystyle\beta_{\tilde{g}_{21}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{g}_{21,R}\left(-\epsilon+4\frac{w\tilde{g}_{12,R}+\tilde{g}_{21,R}}{1+w}+\frac{n+2}{3}\tilde{u}_{1,R}+\frac{n+2}{3}\tilde{u}_{2,R}\right)

(58d)

\displaystyle\beta_{w}

\displaystyle=0

(58e)

where
Eqs. (
58c
)
and (
58d
) have been written to indicate that
\tilde{g}_{12,R}=0
and
\tilde{g}_{21,R}=0
respectively are roots of these
\beta
-functions. Indeed,
\tilde{g}_{12,R}=\tilde{g}_{21,R}=0
correspond to the equilibrium Model B and neither of the two couplings can be generated under the RG from other non-linearities. As the brackets of
Eqs. (
58c
)
and (
58d
) are identical to one loop order, it follows immediately that
\beta_{\tilde{g}_{12}}/\tilde{g}_{12,R}=\beta_{\tilde{g}_{21}}/\tilde{g}_{21,R}
. At the level of the vertex functions, this follows from
\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{1}\phi_{1}\phi_{2}\phi_{2}}^{abcd}(\mathbf{k})/g_{12}=\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{2}\phi_{2}\phi_{1}\phi_{1}}^{abcd}(\mathbf{k})/g_{21}
. As a consequence, the ratio
\sigma_{R}=\tilde{g}_{12,R}/\tilde{g}_{21,R}
,
Eqs. (
8
)
and (
54
), does not flow to one-loop order and will thus be set by the bare values. Similarly,
w_{R}
,
Eqs. (
54
)
and (
58e
), does not flow to one loop. The fixed points of the present model and potentially the scaling exponents, will therefore depend on the bare values of
w
and
\sigma
to one loop.
This “non-universality” resolves at two loops, however, calculating all vertices to two loops is beyond the scope of the present work. For a model similar to ours, Young
et al.

46
;
47
decided to consider the flow only of
w_{R}
as well as
v=\Gamma_{2}/\Gamma_{1}
, which does not flow in the present model to any order, but leave the variable corresponding to
g_{21}/g_{12}=\sigma v
at the bare value.

In the following, we present the analysis of the one-loop flow functions, in particular the dependence of the fixed points and their stability on the ratio between the inter-species coupling
\sigma=\tilde{g}_{21}/\tilde{g}_{12}
and the ratio of diffusivity
w=D_{1}/D_{2}
. In equilibrium, where
\sigma=1
,
Eqs. (
6
)
,
Eq. (
7
)
and
Eq. (
8
)
, corresponding models with bi-quadratic couplings display a rich phase diagram where the stability of the fixed points depend strongly on the number of the field components
n

14
;
15
;
8
. However, it was shown Young
et al.

46
;
47
that for
\sigma\in\{-1,0\}
, the fixed point structure is far simpler. Below, we show how the number of stable fixed points depends on the sign of
\sigma
.

To ease the search for the fixed points, we reproduce in
Eqs. (
59
)
three of the five flow functions in
Eqs. (
58
)
, dropping
\beta_{w}\equiv 0
as well as
\beta_{\tilde{g}_{21}}
, which is essentially identical to
\beta_{\tilde{g}_{12}}
. To one loop, we will need to content ourselves with the presence of the non-universal couplings
\sigma
and
w
.

Restating
Eqs. (
58
)
, we will need to find the roots of

\displaystyle\beta_{\tilde{u}_{1}}

\displaystyle=-\epsilon\tilde{u}_{1,R}+\frac{n+8}{3}\tilde{u}_{1,R}^{2}+3\sigma n\tilde{g}_{12,R}^{2}

(59a)

\displaystyle\beta_{\tilde{u}_{2}}

\displaystyle=-\epsilon\tilde{u}_{2,R}+\frac{n+8}{3}\tilde{u}_{2,R}^{2}+3\sigma n\tilde{g}_{12,R}^{2}

(59b)

\displaystyle\beta_{\tilde{g}_{12}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{g}_{12,R}\left(-\epsilon+4\frac{w+\sigma}{w+1}\tilde{g}_{12,R}+\frac{n+2}{3}\tilde{u}_{1,R}+\frac{n+2}{3}\tilde{u}_{2,R}\right)\ ,

(59c)

and determine the stability of these fixed points, verifying that at a given fixed point the matrix

\Lambda_{ab}=\left.\frac{\partial\beta_{\tilde{\lambda}_{a}}}{\partial\tilde{\lambda}_{b}}\right|_{\tilde{\lambda}_{b}=\tilde{\lambda}^{\star}_{b}}\quad\text{so that}\quad\bm{\Lambda}=\begin{pmatrix}-\epsilon+\frac{2(n+8)}{3}\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}&0&6n\sigma\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}\\
0&-\epsilon+\frac{2(n+8)}{3}\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R}&6n\sigma\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}\\
\frac{1}{3}(n+2)\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}&\frac{1}{3}(n+2)\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}&-\epsilon+8\frac{w+\sigma}{w+1}\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}+\frac{(n+2)}{3}(\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}+\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R})\end{pmatrix}

(60)

for
\tilde{\lambda}_{a}\in\{\tilde{u}_{1,R},\tilde{u}_{2,R},\tilde{g}_{12,R}\}
is positive definite to render it IR stable.

A detailed discussion of the different fixed points
\{\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R},\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R},\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}\}
and their stability is relegated to Appendix
B
. We state the relevant results here. Firstly,

(\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R},\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R},\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R})=\begin{cases}\left(\frac{4-n}{4(n+2)}\frac{h(\sigma,w,n)}{h(\sigma,w,n)+\frac{6}{n+2}}\epsilon+\frac{\epsilon}{4},\frac{4-n}{4(n+2)}\frac{h(\sigma,w,n)}{h(\sigma,w,n)+\frac{6}{n+2}}\epsilon+\frac{\epsilon}{4},\frac{4-n}{4(n+2)}\frac{w+1}{w+\sigma}\frac{\epsilon}{\frac{6}{n+2}+h(\sigma,w,n)}\right),\penalty\ \penalty\ n<4\\
\left(\frac{3\epsilon}{n+8},\frac{3\epsilon}{n+8},0\right),\penalty\ n>4\end{cases}

(61)

with

h(\sigma,w,n)\vcentcolon=\sqrt{1-\sigma\frac{n(4-n)(w+1)^{2}}{4(w+\sigma)^{2}}}.

(62)

is a stable solution for all
\sigma
and
w
, provided only the solution
Eq. (
61
)
is real. As in equilibrium
14
;
15
;
8
, the stability of the fixed points depends on the number of components
n
. For
n>4
and any
\sigma
, at the stable fixed point
Eq. (
61
)
the two order parameter fields
\bm{\phi}_{1}
and
\bm{\phi}_{2}
decouple, as
\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}=0
, so that the present model reduces into two independent Model B. For
n<4
, the fixed points depend non-trivially on the bare couplings
\sigma
and
w
.

Secondly, for
\sigma\in(-\sigma_{+},-\sigma_{-})
, where

\sigma_{\pm}\vcentcolon=\left(w+\frac{n(n+2)^{2}}{8(n+8)}(w+1)^{2}\right)\left(1\pm\sqrt{1-\left(\frac{w}{w+\frac{n(n+2)^{2}}{8(n+8)}(w+1)^{2}}\right)^{2}}\right)\,

(63)

another stable fixed point exists for all
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}

(\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R},\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R},\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R})=\left(\frac{4-n}{4(n+2)}\frac{h(\sigma,w,n)}{h(\sigma,w,n)-\frac{6}{n+2}}\epsilon+\frac{\epsilon}{4},\frac{4-n}{4(n+2)}\frac{h(\sigma,w,n)}{h(\sigma,w,n)-\frac{6}{n+2}}\epsilon+\frac{\epsilon}{4},\frac{4-n}{4(n+2)}\frac{w+1}{w+\sigma}\frac{\epsilon}{\frac{6}{n+2}-h(\sigma,w,n)}\right).

(64)

For all physical parameters values,
w\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
,
\sigma_{\pm}
is greater than unity so that the region
(-\sigma_{+},-\sigma_{-})
always comes to lie on the negative half line. The existence of the additional fixed point,
Eq. (
64
)
suggests that non-reciprocity alters the topology of the one-loop fixed points, inducing other stable points of the RG flow.

III.5
Scaling Exponents

The physics at the fixed points is scale invariant and consequently physical observables typically have a power-law dependence. The scaling exponents of these observables define the universality class of the model and can be used to characterise the system. As usual, we are interested in the scaling behaviour of the response and correlation functions. We expect them to take the following form at the critical point
43
:

\displaystyle\chi_{i}(\mathbf{k},\omega)

\displaystyle=|\mathbf{k}|^{-2+\frac{\eta_{i}+\tilde{\eta}_{i}}{2}}\hat{\chi}_{i}\left(\frac{\omega}{|\mathbf{k}|^{z_{i}}},\frac{r_{1}}{D_{1}|\mathbf{k}|^{1/\nu_{1}}},\frac{r_{2}}{D_{2}|\mathbf{k}|^{1/\nu_{2}}},\ldots\right),

(65a)

\displaystyle C_{i}(\mathbf{k},\omega)

\displaystyle=|\mathbf{k}|^{-2-z_{i}+\eta_{i}}\hat{C}_{i}\left(\frac{\omega}{|\mathbf{k}|^{z_{i}}},\frac{r_{1}}{D_{1}|\mathbf{k}|^{1/\nu_{1}}},\frac{r_{2}}{D_{2}|\mathbf{k}|^{1/\nu_{2}}},\ldots\right),

(65b)

where
\chi_{i}(\mathbf{k},\omega)
and
C_{i}(\mathbf{k},\omega)
are the response and correlation functions for the species
i\in\{1,2\}
, respectively. The universal exponents
\eta_{i}
,
z_{i}
,
\tilde{\eta}_{i}
and
\nu_{i}
have the usual meaning:
z_{i}
is the dynamical exponent for the species
i
, controlling the relative scaling of space and time,
\nu_{i}
controls the divergence of the correlation length as a function of
r_{i}
, and
\eta_{i}
,
\tilde{\eta}_{i}
are anomalous exponents. In the absence of a fluctuation-dissipation theorem, there is no reason why the latter two should equal each other.

In the following, we derive the exponents defined in
Eq. (
65
)
from the Wilson
\gamma
-functions at the fixed points found Section
III.3
. To this end, we write the correlation functions
Eq. (
65
)
in terms of vertex functions
43
,

\displaystyle\chi_{i}(\mathbf{k},\omega)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{k}^{2}/\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{i}\phi_{i}}(\mathbf{k},\omega)

(66a)

\displaystyle C_{i}(\mathbf{k},\omega)

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{i}\tilde{\phi}_{i}}(\mathbf{k},\omega)/|\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{i}\phi_{i}}(\mathbf{k},\omega)|^{2}

(66b)

where we have removed the dependence of the vertices and the correlation functions on the component indices
a
and
b
,
e.g.

Eq. (
46
)
, because vertices and correlation functions are diagonal and the correlation functions draw on the matrix-inverse of the vertex functions,
Eq. (
28
)
.

As a first step, we extract the scaling of the renormalised vertex functions
Eqs. (
36
)
and (
37
) by solving the Callan-Symanzik equation. To this end, we need to make explicit the parameters that the renormalised and the bare vertex functions depend on,
Eq. (
37
)
. It turns out that writing the vertex functions with an explicit dependence on
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
, as well as the corresponding renormalised
\tau_{1,R}
and
\tau_{2,R}
,

Eq. (
34a
)
,
is not convenient, as their flow is coupled,

\mu\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\mu}\begin{pmatrix}\tau_{1,R}(\mu)\\
\tau_{2,R}(\mu)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}-2+\frac{n+2}{3}\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}&nw\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}\\
n\frac{\sigma}{w}\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}&-2+\frac{n+2}{3}\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tau_{1,R}(\mu)\\
\tau_{2,R}(\mu)\end{pmatrix}

(67)

from

Eqs. (
34a
)
, (
56a
) and (
56b
), where the couplings are evaluated at the fixed points of the beta functions
Eqs. (
59
)
. To ease notation, we use that
\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}=\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R}
henceforth.

Denoting the left eigenvectors of the matrix in
Eq. (
67
)
by
\left\langle t_{1}\right|
and
\left\langle t_{2}\right|
with eigenvalues

\gamma_{\tilde{\tau}_{1}}=-2+\frac{n+2}{3}\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}+n\sqrt{\sigma}\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}\penalty\ \text{ and }\penalty\ \gamma_{\tilde{\tau}_{2}}=-2+\frac{n+2}{3}\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}-n\sqrt{\sigma}\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}\ ,

(68)

and correspondingly the right eigenvectors by
\left|t_{1}\right\rangle
and
\left|t_{2}\right\rangle
, we introduce

\displaystyle\tilde{\tau}_{1,R}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\left\langle t_{1}\right|\begin{pmatrix}\tau_{1,R}(\mu)\\
\tau_{2,R}(\mu)\end{pmatrix}

(69a)

\displaystyle\tilde{\tau}_{2,R}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\left\langle t_{2}\right|\begin{pmatrix}\tau_{1,R}(\mu)\\
\tau_{2,R}(\mu)\end{pmatrix}

(69b)

and arrive at the scaling form

\displaystyle\tilde{\tau}_{1,R}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\tau}_{1,R}(\mu_{0})\ell^{\gamma_{\tilde{\tau}_{1}}}

(70a)

\displaystyle\tilde{\tau}_{2,R}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\tau}_{2,R}(\mu_{0})\ell^{\gamma_{\tilde{\tau}_{2}}}

(70b)

with
\ell=\mu/\mu_{0}
henceforth.

Introducing the vertex functions with the full set of parameters along the lines of
Eq. (
31
)
and (
37
), say

Z_{\bm{\tilde{\phi}}_{i}}(\mu)Z_{\bm{\phi}_{i}}(\mu)\Gamma_{R,\tilde{\phi}_{i}\phi_{i}}^{ab}\left(\tilde{\tau}_{1,R}(\mu),\tilde{\tau}_{2,R}(\mu),D_{1,R}(\mu),D_{2,R}(\mu),\Gamma_{1,R}(\mu),\Gamma_{2,R}(\mu),\tilde{u}_{1,R}(\mu),\tilde{u}_{2,R}(\mu),\tilde{g}_{12,R}(\mu),\sigma_{R}(\mu);\{\mathbf{k}\},\{\omega\};\mu\right)\\
=\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{i}\phi_{i}}^{ab}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},D_{1},D_{2},\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},u_{1},u_{2},g_{12},g_{21};\{\mathbf{k}\},\{\omega\}),

(71)

with some of the parameters on the left defined via
Eq. (
54
)
.

Eq. (
71
)
leads us to the Callan-Symanzik equation
25
by differentiation with respect to
\mu
on both sides. It is solved by the method of characteristics and leads to

\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{\tilde{\phi}_{i}}+\gamma^{*}_{\phi_{i}}}\Gamma^{ab}_{R,\tilde{\phi}_{i}\phi_{i}}\big(\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{\tilde{\tau}_{1}}}\tilde{\tau}_{1,R}(\mu_{0}),\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{\tilde{\tau}_{2}}}\tilde{\tau}_{2,R}(\mu_{0}),\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{D_{1}}}D_{1,R}(\mu_{0}),\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{D_{2}}}D_{2,R}(\mu_{0}),\\
\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{\Gamma_{1}}}\Gamma_{1,R}(\mu_{0}),\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{\Gamma_{2}}}\Gamma_{2,R}(\mu_{0}),\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R},\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R},\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R},\sigma^{*}_{R};\{\mathbf{k}\},\{\omega\};\ell\mu_{0}\big)\\
=\Gamma_{R,\tilde{\phi}_{i}\phi_{i}}^{ab}\left(\tilde{\tau}_{1,R}(\mu_{0}),\tilde{\tau}_{2,R}(\mu_{0}),D_{1,R}(\mu_{0}),D_{2,R}(\mu_{0}),\Gamma_{1,R}(\mu_{0}),\Gamma_{2,R}(\mu_{0}),\tilde{u}_{1,R}(\mu_{0}),\tilde{u}_{2,R}(\mu_{0}),\tilde{g}_{12,R}(\mu_{0}),\sigma_{R}(\mu_{0});\{\mathbf{k}\},\{\omega\};\mu_{0}\right)

(72)

close to the critical point, where

\displaystyle\gamma_{\phi_{i}}

\displaystyle=\mu\frac{\partial\log Z_{\phi_{i}}}{\partial\mu}\qquad\gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{i}}=\mu\frac{\partial\log Z_{\tilde{\phi}_{i}}}{\partial\mu},

(73a)

\displaystyle\gamma_{D_{i}}

\displaystyle=\mu\frac{\partial\log D_{i,R}}{\partial\mu}=\mu\frac{\partial\log Z_{D_{i}}}{\partial\mu}

(73b)

\displaystyle\gamma_{\Gamma_{i}}

\displaystyle=\mu\frac{\partial\log\Gamma_{i,R}}{\partial\mu}=\mu\frac{\partial\log Z_{\Gamma_{i}}}{\partial\mu}

(73c)

where
\gamma_{\tilde{\tau}_{i}}
are stated in
Eq. (
68
)
and

\gamma_{\phi_{i}}
,
\gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{i}}
and
\gamma_{\Gamma_{i}}
are determined only in the form

\displaystyle\gamma_{\phi_{i}}+\gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{i}}

\displaystyle=0

(74a)

\displaystyle\gamma_{\Gamma_{i}}+2\gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{i}}

\displaystyle=0

(74b)

due to the over-parameterisation that allows us to introduce two independent dimensions
B_{1}
and
B_{2}
above.

Eq. (
72
)
relates the renormalised vertex function at different scales,
\mu=\ell\mu_{0}
on the left and
\mu_{0}
on the right. We may think of
\mu_{0}
at being a scale at which we can evaluate our field theory safely by a perturbation theory. Close to the critical point the three couplings
\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}
,
\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R}
and
\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}
attain their fixed point values, Section
III.4
, and similarly
w^{*}_{R}
and
\sigma^{*}_{R}
, which to one loop do not flow and therefore keep their bare values.

Based on the dimensional analysis in Section
II.2
we further have that

\Gamma_{R,\tilde{\phi}_{i}\phi_{i}}^{ab}\left(\tilde{\tau}_{1,R},\tilde{\tau}_{2,R},D_{1,R},D_{2,R},\Gamma_{1,R},\Gamma_{2,R},\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R},\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R},\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R},\sigma^{*}_{R};\{\mathbf{k}\},\{\omega\};\mu\right)\\
=AL^{-4}\Gamma_{R,\tilde{\phi}_{i}\phi_{i}}^{ab}\left(\frac{\tilde{\tau}_{1,R}}{A},\frac{\tilde{\tau}_{2,R}}{A},\frac{D_{1,R}}{A},\frac{D_{2,R}}{A},\frac{\Gamma_{1,R}}{B_{1}},\frac{\Gamma_{2,R}}{B_{2}},\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R},\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R},\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R},\sigma^{*}_{R};\{\mathbf{k}L\},\left\{\frac{\omega L^{4}}{A}\right\};\mu L\right)

(75)

holds for any
A,B_{1},B_{2},L\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
. Using this on the left-hand side of
Eq. (
72
)
with
diffusive scale
A=D_{i,R}(\mu_{0})\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{D_{i}}}
,
noise scale
B_{i}=\Gamma_{i,R}(\mu_{0})\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{\Gamma_{i}}}
and length scale
L=1/\ell
then gives the desired scaling form

\Gamma_{R,\tilde{\phi}_{i}\phi_{i}}^{ab}\left(\tilde{\tau}_{1,R}(\mu_{0}),\tilde{\tau}_{2,R}(\mu_{0}),D_{1,R}(\mu_{0}),D_{2,R}(\mu_{0}),\Gamma_{1,R}(\mu_{0}),\Gamma_{2,R}(\mu_{0}),\tilde{u}_{1,R}(\mu_{0}),\tilde{u}_{2,R}(\mu_{0}),\tilde{g}_{12,R}(\mu_{0}),\sigma_{R}(\mu_{0});\{\mathbf{k}\},\{\omega\};\mu_{0}\right)\\
=D_{i,R}(\mu_{0})\ell^{4+\gamma^{*}_{\tilde{\phi}_{i}}+\gamma^{*}_{\phi_{i}}+\gamma^{*}_{D_{i}}}\Gamma^{ab}_{R,\tilde{\phi}_{i}\phi_{i}}\big(\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{\tilde{\tau}_{1}}-\gamma^{*}_{D_{i}}}\frac{\tilde{\tau}_{1,R}(\mu_{0})}{D_{i,R}(\mu_{0})},\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{\tilde{\tau}_{2}}-\gamma^{*}_{D_{i}}}\frac{\tilde{\tau}_{2,R}(\mu_{0})}{D_{i,R}(\mu_{0})},\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{D_{1}}-\gamma^{*}_{D_{i}}}\frac{D_{1,R}(\mu)}{D_{i,R}(\mu)},\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{D_{2}}-\gamma^{*}_{D_{i}}}\frac{D_{2,R}(\mu)}{D_{i,R}(\mu)},\\
1,1,\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R},\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R},\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R},\sigma^{*}_{R};\{\ell^{-1}\mathbf{k}\},\left\{\frac{\omega}{D_{i,R}(\mu_{0})\ell^{4+\gamma^{*}_{D_{i}}}}\right\};\mu_{0}\big)\ .

(76)

The choice
A=D_{i,R}(\mu_{0})\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{D_{1}}}
makes obvious that the two fields are characterised by two different dynamical exponents,
Eq. (
65a
)

z_{i}=4+\gamma^{*}_{D_{i}}=4+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})

(77)

with
Eq. (
73b
)
, as can be gleaned from the scaling of
\omega
with
\ell
. Further, from the prefactor
\ell^{4+\gamma^{*}_{\tilde{\phi}_{i}}+\gamma^{*}_{\phi_{i}}+\gamma^{*}_{D_{i}}}=\ell^{4-\eta_{i}/2-\tilde{\eta}_{i}/2}
and
Eqs. (
73b
)
and (
74a
) we have that
\eta_{i}+\tilde{\eta}_{i}=-2\gamma^{*}_{D_{i}}=0+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})
. Even though
\eta_{i}+\tilde{\eta}_{i}
is zero at one-loop level, we find that it is directly dependent on the dynamical scaling exponent

z_{i}=4-\frac{\eta_{i}+\tilde{\eta}_{i}}{2},

(78)

a property shared by models with conserved dynamics. As for the divergence of the correlation lengths, in principle there are four exponents, but once a particular scaling form is assumed, we may reduce them to two. For the present scaling forms,
Eq. (
65a
)
, we have

1/\nu_{1}=-\gamma^{*}_{\tilde{\tau}_{1}}+\gamma^{*}_{D_{1}}
and
1/\nu_{2}=-\gamma^{*}_{\tilde{\tau}_{2}}+\gamma^{*}_{D_{2}}
. To one loop, however,
\gamma^{*}_{D_{1}}=\gamma^{*}_{D_{2}}=0
, so that

\displaystyle\nu_{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\gamma^{*}_{\tilde{\tau}_{1}}}=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{n+2}{12}\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}+\frac{n\sqrt{\sigma}}{4}\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}

(79a)

\displaystyle\nu_{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\gamma^{*}_{\tilde{\tau}_{2}}}=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{n+2}{12}\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}-\frac{n\sqrt{\sigma}}{4}\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}

(79b)

from
Eq. (
68
)
to be evaluated with
\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}
,
\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R}
,
\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}
from
Eqs. (
61
)
or (
64
) and with
\sigma
to one loop determined at bare level.

A similar analysis can be performed for the correlation function,
\langle\phi^{a}_{i}(\mathbf{k},\omega)\phi^{b}_{i}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\rangle=\delta^{ab}\delta\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime})\delta\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22(\omega+\omega^{\prime})C_{i}(\mathbf{k},\omega)
, to determine
\eta_{i}
and
\tilde{\eta}_{i}
independently. Similar to
Eq. (
71
)
, we introduce the renormalised correlation function
Eq. (
66b
)

with the full set of renormalised parameters

Z^{-2}_{\bm{\phi}_{i}}(\mu)C_{i,R}(\tilde{\tau}_{1,R}(\mu),\tilde{\tau}_{2,R}(\mu),D_{1,R}(\mu),D_{2,R}(\mu),\Gamma_{1,R}(\mu),\Gamma_{2,R}(\mu),\tilde{u}_{1,R}(\mu),\tilde{u}_{2,R}(\mu),\tilde{g}_{12,R}(\mu),\sigma_{R}(\mu);\{\mathbf{k}\},\{\omega\};\mu)\\
=C_{i}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},D_{1},D_{2},\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},u_{1},u_{2},g_{12},g_{21};\{\mathbf{k}\},\{\omega\}),

(80)

with some of the arguments of the renormalised correlation function defined in
Eq. (
54
)
. Differentiating the LHS of
Eq. (
80
)
with respect to
\mu
leads us to another Callan-Symanzik equation, now for the correlation function, which can also be solved by method of characteristics as before, leading to

\ell^{-2\gamma^{*}_{\phi_{i}}}C_{i,R}\big(\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{\tilde{\tau}_{1}}}\tilde{\tau}_{1,R}(\mu_{0}),\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{\tilde{\tau}_{2}}}\tilde{\tau}_{2,R}(\mu_{0}),\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{D_{1}}}D_{1,R}(\mu_{0}),\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{D_{2}}}D_{2,R}(\mu_{0}),\\
\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{\Gamma_{1}}}\Gamma_{1,R}(\mu_{0}),\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{\Gamma_{2}}}\Gamma_{2,R}(\mu_{0}),\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R},\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R},\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R},\sigma^{*}_{R};\{\mathbf{k}\},\{\omega\};\ell\mu_{0}\big)\\
=C_{i,R}\big(\tilde{\tau}_{1,R}(\mu_{0}),\tilde{\tau}_{2,R}(\mu_{0}),D_{1,R}(\mu_{0}),D_{2,R}(\mu_{0}),\Gamma_{1,R}(\mu_{0}),\Gamma_{2,R}(\mu_{0}),\tilde{u}_{1,R}(\mu_{0}),\tilde{u}_{2,R}(\mu_{0}),\tilde{g}_{12,R}(\mu_{0}),\sigma_{R}(\mu_{0});\{\mathbf{k}\},\{\omega\};\mu_{0})

(81)

close to the critical point. There, the couplings
\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}
,
\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R}
and
\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}
reach their fixed point values, while
w^{*}_{R}
and
\sigma^{*}_{R}
retain their bare values as they do not flow to one loop, so that the Wilson gamma functions,
Eq. (
73
)
, can be treated as constants.

Based on dimensional analysis in Section
II.2
we further have that

C_{i,R}(\tilde{\tau}_{1,R},\tilde{\tau}_{2,R},D_{1,R},D_{2,R},\Gamma_{1,R},\Gamma_{2,R},\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R},\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R},\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R},\sigma^{*}_{R};\{\mathbf{k}\},\{\omega\};\mu)\\
=B_{i}A^{-2}L^{6}C_{i,R}\bigg(\frac{\tilde{\tau}_{1,R}}{A},\frac{\tilde{\tau}_{2,R}}{A},\frac{D_{1,R}}{A},\frac{D_{2,R}}{A},\frac{\Gamma_{1,R}}{B_{1}},\frac{\Gamma_{2,R}}{B_{2}},\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R},\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R},\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R},\sigma^{*}_{R};\{\mathbf{k}L\},\left\{\frac{\omega L^{4}}{A}\right\};\mu L\bigg)

(82)

which holds for any
A,B_{1},B_{2},L\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
. Once again, using this on the LHS of
Eq. (
81
)
with the diffusive scale
A=D_{i,R}(\mu_{0})\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{D_{i}}}
, noise scale
B_{i}=\Gamma_{i,R}(\mu_{0})\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{\Gamma_{i}}}
and length scale
L=1/\ell
gives the desired scaling form

C_{i,R}(\tilde{\tau}_{1,R}(\mu_{0}),\tilde{\tau}_{2,R}(\mu_{0}),D_{1,R}(\mu_{0}),D_{2,R}(\mu_{0}),\Gamma_{1,R}(\mu_{0}),\Gamma_{2,R}(\mu_{0}),\tilde{u}_{1,R}(\mu_{0}),\tilde{u}_{2,R}(\mu_{0}),\tilde{g}_{12,R}(\mu_{0}),\sigma_{R}(\mu_{0});\{\mathbf{k}\},\{\omega\};\mu_{0})\\
=\frac{\Gamma_{i,R}(\mu_{0})}{D^{2}_{i,R}(\mu_{0})}\ell^{-6+\gamma^{*}_{\Gamma_{i}}-2\gamma^{*}_{\bm{\phi}_{i}}-2\gamma^{*}_{D_{i}}}C_{i,R}\bigg(\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{\tilde{\tau}_{1}}-\gamma^{*}_{D_{i}}}\frac{\tilde{\tau}_{1,R}(\mu_{0})}{D_{i,R}(\mu_{0})},\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{\tilde{\tau}_{2}}-\gamma^{*}_{D_{i}}}\frac{\tilde{\tau}_{2,R}(\mu_{0})}{D_{i,R}(\mu_{0})},\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{D_{1}}-\gamma^{*}_{D_{i}}}\frac{D_{1,R}(\mu)}{D_{i,R}(\mu)},\ell^{\gamma^{*}_{D_{2}}-\gamma^{*}_{D_{i}}}\frac{D_{2,R}(\mu)}{D_{i,R}(\mu)},\\
1,1,\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R},\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R},\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R},\sigma^{*}_{R};\{\ell^{-1}\mathbf{k}\},\left\{\frac{\omega}{D_{i,R}(\mu_{0})\ell^{4+\gamma^{*}_{D_{i}}}}\right\};\mu_{0}\bigg)\ .

(83)

The prefactor
\ell^{-6+\gamma^{*}_{\Gamma_{i}}-2\gamma^{*}_{\bm{\phi}_{i}}-2\gamma^{*}_{D_{i}}}
can be simplified by employing
Eqs. (
74
)
, whereby
\gamma^{*}_{\Gamma_{i}}-2\gamma^{*}_{\bm{\phi}_{i}}=0
and we can individually identify
\eta_{i}
and
\tilde{\eta}_{i}
by comparing the prefactor,
\ell^{-6+\gamma^{*}_{\Gamma_{i}}-2\gamma^{*}_{\bm{\phi}_{i}}-2\gamma^{*}_{D_{i}}}
, with
Eq. (
65b
)
and using the value of the dynamical scaling exponent
Eq. (
77
)

\eta_{i}=\tilde{\eta}_{i}=-\gamma^{*}_{D_{i}}=0+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2}).

(84)

Even though the present non-reciprocal Model B breaks detailed balance, we therefore find that
\tilde{\eta}_{i}=\eta_{i}
to all orders in the perturbative expansion, as
-2-z_{i}+\eta_{i}=-6-2\gamma^{*}_{D_{i}}
,
\eta_{i}+\tilde{\eta}_{i}=-2\gamma^{*}_{D_{i}}
and
z_{i}=4-(\eta_{i}+\tilde{\eta}_{i})/2
,
Eq. (
78
)
. The equality
\eta_{i}=\tilde{\eta}_{i}
thus goes back to
Eq. (
74
)
and thus to
Eq. (
44
)
,
i.e.
the conserved nature of the dynamics, while in equilibrium
Eq. (
44
)
is a result of a fluctuation-dissipation relation.
The equality of
\tilde{\eta}
and
\eta
may thus be considered a generic feature of models with conserved dynamics, regardless of whether the underlying dynamics obeys detailed balance.

IV
Conclusion

In this paper, we have considered the critical dynamics of two conserved order parameter fields with non-reciprocal interactions. Unlike typical implementations of non-reciprocity
16
;
1
;
9
;
6
;
7
;
45
;
37
;
31
;
35
;
34
;
42
which induce dynamical steady states and spatio-temporal patterns, we have chosen an implementation where the non-reciprocity enters only through the non-linear coupling between the two order parameter fields. After casting the model into an MSR field theory, we have constructed the perturbative expansion and used it to carry out the field-theoretic RG procedure to one loop. The results of the RG calculation reveal the exponents to first order in
\epsilon=4-d
, namely
z_{i}

Eqs. (
77
)
,
\nu_{i}
(
79
) and
\eta_{i},\tilde{\eta}_{i}
(
84
). A two-loop analysis is needed to determine the non-trivial behaviour of
z_{i}
and
\eta_{i},\tilde{\eta}_{i}
for
n<4
,
as several couplings,
w
and
\sigma
, do not receive any correction and retain their bare value under the RG. For
n>4
, however, at one stable fixed point
Eq. (
61
)
the fields decouple and they individually undergo Model B equilibrium dynamics of Hohenberg and Halperin’s classification
19
.
Finally, by using the conservation of the order parameter fields, we have shown that even if the detailed balance is broken through non-reciprocal couplings, the scaling of correlation and response functions of each field will be characterised by a single anomalous exponent
\eta_{i}=\tilde{\eta}_{i}

Eq. (
84
)
, that is also linearly dependent on the dynamical scaling exponent,
z_{i}
,
Eq. (
78
)
.

The present work opens a number of interesting new avenues: Firstly, it is necessary to perform a
full
two-loop analysis and verify the existence of the proposed non-equilibrium fixed points of
46
;
47
. Secondly, one may generalise the present setup to the fields having different number of components,
\bm{\phi}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}}
and
\bm{\phi}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{2}}
.
47
observed this to break the strong dynamical scaling
z_{1}=z_{2}
and cause non-trivial scaling exponents. Finally, for
n_{1}=n_{2}=n
, it would be intriguing to introduce non-reciprocity between the two fields at the
linear
level, providing a way to promote the underlying symmetry from
O(n)\otimes O(n)
to
U(n)
, and explore the effect on the Wilson-Fisher fixed point.
42
showed that for
n=1
such systems can elegantly be written in terms of a single
scalar
complex order parameter field. Applying their approach to the present setup, one would repeat our RG analysis with
complex
order parameter vector fields where the non-reciprocity is encoded in the coupling constants acquiring a imaginary component.

Acknowledgments

E.S. would like to thank Giulia Pisegna and Martin Johnsrud for interesting conversations. E.S. is supported by Roth PhD scholarships funded by the Department of Mathematics at Imperial College London.

Appendix A
Details of the Loop Calculation

In this section, we list and evaluate the amputated loop diagrams that renormalise the relevant vertices. The field theory defined by Eqs. (
19
) and (
20
) is closed under RG flow, meaning no terms that are not already present in the action will be generated by the RG procedure. In what follows, we focus on the one-loop diagrams.
Overall, there are
16
such diagrams. However, we need to calculate only eight of them since the other half can be calculated easily by swapping the indices of the species
1
and
2
. We proceed vertex-by-vertex.

\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{1}\phi_{1}}^{ab}(\mathbf{k},\omega)

{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}}{\lxSVG@fill\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 50.56 6.29 L 59.51 10.56 L 53.87 15.64 Z}{stroke:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(\mathbf{k},\omega)

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\int\mathchoice{\!\text{\dj}^{d}k\,}{\!\text{\dj}^{d}k\,}{\!\text{\dj}^{d}k\,}{\!\text{\dj}^{d}k\,}_{1}\int\mathchoice{\!\text{\dj}\omega_{1}\,}{\!\text{\dj}\omega_{1}\,}{\!\text{\dj}\omega_{1}\,}{\!\text{\dj}\omega_{1}\,}\frac{2\Gamma_{2}\mathbf{k}^{2}_{1}}{(-\mathring{\imath}\omega_{1}+\Delta_{2}(\mathbf{k}_{1}))(\mathring{\imath}\omega_{1}+\Delta_{2}(\mathbf{k}_{1}))},

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma_{2}}{D_{2}}\int\mathchoice{\!\text{\dj}^{d}k_{1}\,}{\!\text{\dj}^{d}k_{1}\,}{\!\text{\dj}^{d}k_{1}\,}{\!\text{\dj}^{d}k_{1}\,}\frac{1}{\mathbf{k}^{2}_{1}+r_{2}/D_{2}},

(87b)

using the propagators in
Eqs. (
23
)
with
\Delta_{i}(\mathbf{k})=\mathbf{k}^{2}(r_{i}+D_{i}\mathbf{k}^{2})
. To one loop, as generically found in field theories with only quartic non-linearities, only the mass and not the diffusion constant get corrected. The quadratic divergences in
Eqs. (
87
)
require an additive renormalisation, so that the critical point
r_{1,c}
moves away from the mean-field value. Demanding
\left.\frac{\partial\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{1}\phi_{1}}^{ab}(\mathbf{k},\omega)}{\partial\mathbf{k}^{2}}\right|_{\mathbf{k}=0,\omega=0}=0
at
r_{1}=r_{1,c}
in
Eq. (
86
)
, self-consistency requires

\displaystyle r_{1,c}

\displaystyle=-u_{1}\frac{n+2}{6}\frac{\Gamma_{1}}{D_{1}}\int\text{\dj}^{d}k_{1}\frac{1}{\mathbf{k}^{2}_{1}+r_{1,c}/D_{1}}-g_{12}\frac{n}{2}\frac{\Gamma_{2}}{D_{2}}\int\text{\dj}^{d}k_{1}\frac{1}{\mathbf{k}^{2}_{1}+r_{2,c}/D_{2}}

\displaystyle=-u_{1}\frac{n+2}{6}\frac{\Gamma_{1}}{D_{1}}\int\text{\dj}^{d}k_{1}\frac{1}{\mathbf{k}^{2}_{1}}-g_{12}\frac{n}{2}\frac{\Gamma_{2}}{D_{2}}\int\text{\dj}^{d}k_{1}\frac{1}{\mathbf{k}^{2}_{1}}+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{2})\ .

(88)

Parameterising the field theory in terms of
\tau_{i}=r_{i}-r_{i,c}
,
\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{1}\phi_{1}}^{ab}(\mathbf{k},\omega)
to one loop can be written as

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{1}\phi_{1}}^{ab}(\mathbf{k},\omega)

\displaystyle=\left[-\mathring{\imath}\omega+D_{1}\mathbf{k}^{4}+\mathbf{k}^{2}\left(\tau_{1}+r_{1,c}+u_{1}\frac{n+2}{6}\frac{\Gamma_{1}}{D_{1}}\int\text{\dj}^{d}k_{1}\frac{1}{\mathbf{k}^{2}_{1}+(\tau_{1}+r_{1,c})/D_{1}}+g_{12}\frac{n}{2}\frac{\Gamma_{2}}{D_{2}}\int\text{\dj}^{d}k_{1}\frac{1}{\mathbf{k}^{2}_{1}+(\tau_{2}+r_{2,c})/D_{2}}\right)\right]\delta^{ab}

\displaystyle=\left[-\mathring{\imath}\omega+D_{1}\mathbf{k}^{4}+\tau_{1}\mathbf{k}^{2}\left(1-u_{1}\frac{n+2}{6}\frac{\Gamma_{1}}{D_{1}^{2}}\int\text{\dj}^{d}k_{1}\frac{1}{\mathbf{k}^{2}_{1}(\mathbf{k}^{2}_{1}+\tau_{1}/D_{1})}-g_{12}\frac{n}{2}\frac{\Gamma_{2}}{D_{2}^{2}}\frac{\tau_{2}}{\tau_{1}}\int\text{\dj}^{d}k_{1}\frac{1}{\mathbf{k}^{2}_{1}(\mathbf{k}^{2}_{1}+\tau_{2}/D_{2})}\right)\right]\delta^{ab}

\displaystyle=\left[-\mathring{\imath}\omega+D_{1}\mathbf{k}^{4}+\tau_{1}\mathbf{k}^{2}\left(1-u_{1}\frac{n+2}{6}\frac{\Gamma_{1}}{D_{1}^{2}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\epsilon/2\right)}{(4\pi)^{d/2}}\left(1-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{\tau_{1}}{D_{1}}\right)^{-\epsilon/2}-g_{12}\frac{n}{2}\frac{\Gamma_{2}}{D_{2}^{2}}\frac{\tau_{2}}{\tau_{1}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\epsilon/2\right)}{(4\pi)^{d/2}}\left(1-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{\tau_{2}}{D_{2}}\right)^{-\epsilon/2}\right)\right]\delta^{ab}

(89)

where we have used the definition of
r_{1,c}

Eq. (
A
)
from the second to the third line to leading order in the non-linearities, thereby omitting
r_{1,c}
in the integrands.

Applying the same procedure to
\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{2}\phi_{2}}^{ab}(\mathbf{k},\omega)
,

(90)

we arrive at

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{2}\phi_{2}}^{ab}(\mathbf{k},\omega)=

\displaystyle=\left[-\mathring{\imath}\omega+D_{2}\mathbf{k}^{4}+\tau_{2}\mathbf{k}^{2}\left(1-u_{2}\frac{n+2}{6}\frac{\Gamma_{2}}{D_{2}^{2}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\epsilon/2\right)}{(4\pi)^{d/2}}\left(1-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{\tau_{2}}{D_{2}}\right)^{-\epsilon/2}-g_{21}\frac{n}{2}\frac{\Gamma_{1}}{D_{1}^{2}}\frac{\tau_{1}}{\tau_{2}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\epsilon/2\right)}{(4\pi)^{d/2}}\left(1-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{\tau_{1}}{D_{1}}\right)^{-\epsilon/2}\right)\right]\delta^{ab}

(91)

(92)

where
sym
refers to the diagrams generated by permutations of the incoming legs, whose components are labelled by
b
,
c
and
d
. As can be seen by the double dashes on the outgoing (amputated) legs, every diagram is multiplied by the outgoing
\mathbf{k}^{2}
. We are interested only in such terms proportional to
\mathbf{k}^{2}
, which allows us safely set
\mathbf{k}_{i}=\mathbf{0}
inside the diagrams, as the additional contributions produced by
\mathbf{k}_{i}\neq\mathbf{0}
are UV-convergent. We thus arrive at

\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{1}\phi_{1}\phi_{1}\phi_{1}}^{abcd}(\mathbf{k})=-\mathbf{k}^{2}\frac{Q^{abcd}}{3}\left\{u_{1}-u_{1}^{2}\frac{n+8}{3}\mathcal{J}_{\hbox to36.14pt{\vbox to36.14pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-26.3144pt\lower-1.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\int\text{\dj}^{d}k_{1}\int\text{\dj}\omega_{1}\frac{2\Gamma_{1}\mathbf{k}_{1}^{4}}{(-\mathring{\imath}\omega_{1}+\Delta_{1}(\mathbf{k}_{1}))(\mathring{\imath}\omega_{1}+\Delta_{1}(\mathbf{k}_{1}))^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma_{1}}{D_{1}^{2}}\int\text{\dj}^{d}k_{1}\frac{1}{(\mathbf{k}^{2}_{1}+\tau_{1}/D_{1})^{2}}=\frac{\Gamma_{1}}{D_{1}^{2}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\epsilon/2\right)}{(4\pi)^{d/2}}\left(\frac{\tau_{1}}{D_{1}}\right)^{-\epsilon/2}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\int\text{\dj}^{d}k_{1}\int\text{\dj}\omega_{1}\frac{2\Gamma_{2}\mathbf{k}_{1}^{4}}{(-\mathring{\imath}\omega_{1}+\Delta_{2}(\mathbf{k}_{1}))(i\omega_{1}+\Delta_{2}(\mathbf{k}_{1}))^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma_{2}}{D_{2}^{2}}\int\text{\dj}^{d}k_{1}\frac{1}{(\mathbf{k}^{2}_{1}+\tau_{2}/D_{2})^{2}}=\frac{\Gamma_{2}}{D_{2}^{2}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\epsilon/2\right)}{(4\pi)^{d/2}}\left(\frac{\tau_{2}}{D_{2}}\right)^{-\epsilon/2}\ ,

(94b)

using the renormalised inverse propagator
-\mathring{\imath}\omega+\Delta_{i}(\mathbf{k})=-\mathring{\imath}\omega+\mathbf{k}^{2}(\tau_{i}+D_{i}\mathbf{k}^{2})
.
Substituting
Eqs. (
94
)
into
Eq. (
93
)
, to one loop, the vertex therefore takes the form

\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{1}\phi_{1}\phi_{1}\phi_{1}}^{abcd}(\mathbf{k})=-\mathbf{k}^{2}u_{1}\frac{Q^{abcd}}{3}\left[1-u_{1}\frac{n+8}{6}\frac{\Gamma_{1}}{D_{1}^{2}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\epsilon/2\right)}{(4\pi)^{d/2}}\left(\frac{\tau_{1}}{D_{1}}\right)^{-\epsilon/2}-\frac{g_{12}g_{21}}{u_{1}}\frac{3n}{2}\frac{\Gamma_{2}}{D_{2}^{2}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\epsilon/2\right)}{(4\pi)^{d/2}}\left(\frac{\tau_{2}}{D_{2}}\right)^{-\epsilon/2}\right]+\mathcal{O}(\mathbf{k}^{4})

(95)

The vertex
\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{2}\phi_{2}\phi_{2}\phi_{2}}^{abcd}(\mathbf{k})
follows the same pattern,

These are the same diagrams as those that renormalise
\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{1}\phi_{1}\phi_{1}\phi_{1}}^{abcd}(\mathbf{k})
,
Eq. (
94
)
, but with solid and dashed lines swapped. Therefore, the contribution to the vertex
\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{2}\phi_{2}\phi_{2}\phi_{2}}^{abcd}(\mathbf{k})
will be that of
\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{1}\phi_{1}\phi_{1}\phi_{1}}^{abcd}(\mathbf{k})
with indices
1
and
2
swapped,
Eq. (
95
)
,

\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{2}\phi_{2}\phi_{2}\phi_{2}}^{abcd}(\mathbf{k})=-\mathbf{k}^{2}u_{2}\frac{Q^{abcd}}{3}\left[1-u_{2}\frac{n+8}{6}\frac{\Gamma_{2}}{D_{2}^{2}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\epsilon/2\right)}{(4\pi)^{d/2}}\left(\frac{\tau_{2}}{D_{2}}\right)^{-\epsilon/2}-\frac{g_{12}g_{21}}{u_{2}}\frac{3n}{2}\frac{\Gamma_{1}}{D_{1}^{2}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\epsilon/2\right)}{(4\pi)^{d/2}}\left(\frac{\tau_{1}}{D_{1}}\right)^{-\epsilon/2}\right]+\mathcal{O}(\mathbf{k}^{4})

(97)

\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{1}\phi_{1}\phi_{2}\phi_{2}}^{abcd}(\mathbf{k})
. The one loop diagrams that contribute to it are given by

(102)

These are the same diagrams as those that renormalise
\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{1}\phi_{1}\phi_{2}\phi_{2}}^{abcd}(\mathbf{k})
but with solid and dashed lines swapped. This means that the contribution to the vertex
\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{2}\phi_{2}\phi_{1}\phi_{1}}^{abcd}(\mathbf{k})
will be that of
\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{1}\phi_{1}\phi_{2}\phi_{2}}^{abcd}(\mathbf{k})
,
Eq. (
101
)
, with indices
1
and
2
swapped:

\Gamma_{\tilde{\phi}_{2}\phi_{2}\phi_{1}\phi_{1}}^{abcd}(\mathbf{k})=-\mathbf{k}^{2}g_{21}\delta^{ab}\delta^{cd}\left[1-u_{1}\frac{n+2}{6}\frac{\Gamma_{1}}{D_{1}^{2}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\epsilon/2\right)}{(4\pi)^{d/2}}\left(\frac{\tau_{1}}{D_{1}}\right)^{-\epsilon/2}-u_{2}\frac{n+2}{6}\frac{\Gamma_{2}}{D_{2}^{2}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\epsilon/2\right)}{(4\pi)^{d/2}}\left(\frac{\tau_{2}}{D_{2}}\right)^{-\epsilon/2}\right.\\
\left.-\frac{2g_{12}\Gamma_{2}}{D_{2}(D_{1}+D_{2})}\frac{\Gamma\left(\epsilon/2\right)}{(4\pi)^{d/2}}\left(\frac{\tau_{2}}{D_{2}}\right)^{-\epsilon/2}\Big(1+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)\Big)-\frac{2g_{21}\Gamma_{1}}{D_{1}(D_{1}+D_{2})}\frac{\Gamma\left(\epsilon/2\right)}{(4\pi)^{d/2}}\left(\frac{\tau_{1}}{D_{1}}\right)^{-\epsilon/2}\Big(1+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)\Big)\right]

(103)

This completes the derivations in the present appendix. Calculating the
Z
-factors,
Eqs. (
56
)
, and the Wilson
\gamma
-functions,
e.g.

Eqs. (
73
)
, from it, is a matter of collecting terms.
4

Appendix B
Analysis of the one-loop flow functions

In this section, we perform a detailed analysis of the flow functions given in
Eqs. (
59
)
,

\displaystyle\beta_{\tilde{u}_{1}}

\displaystyle=-\epsilon\tilde{u}_{1,R}+\frac{n+8}{3}\tilde{u}_{1,R}^{2}+3\sigma n\tilde{g}_{12,R}^{2}

(104a)

\displaystyle\beta_{\tilde{u}_{2}}

\displaystyle=-\epsilon\tilde{u}_{2,R}+\frac{n+8}{3}\tilde{u}_{2,R}^{2}+3\sigma n\tilde{g}_{12,R}^{2}

(104b)

\displaystyle\beta_{\tilde{g}_{12}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{g}_{12,R}\left(-\epsilon+4\frac{w+\sigma}{w+1}\tilde{g}_{12,R}+\frac{n+2}{3}\tilde{u}_{1,R}+\frac{n+2}{3}\tilde{u}_{2,R}\right)\ ,

(104c)

and derive the results quoted in the main text, in particular the precise values of the two stable fixed points
Eqs. (
61
)
and (
64
).

We start our analysis by classifying the possible solutions. As a first criterion, we note that
\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}=0
is always a solution of
\beta_{\tilde{g}_{12}}=0
,
Eq. (
104c
)
, whereas the solution of
\beta_{\tilde{u}_{1}}=0
depends on both
u_{1}
and
g_{1}2
, and similarly for
\beta_{\tilde{u}_{2}}=0
. We therefore categorise the solution by triviality of
\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}
. As a second criterion, subtracting
\beta_{\tilde{u}_{2}}
from
\beta_{\tilde{u}_{1}}
, we find that

\left(\frac{n+8}{3}(\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}+\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R})-\epsilon\right)(\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}-\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R})=0

(105)

from which we infer that either
\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}=\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R}
or
\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}+\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R}=3\epsilon/(n+8)
. These criteria, firstly
\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}
vanishing or not and secondly
\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}=\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R}
or
\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}+\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R}=3\epsilon/(n+8)
(or both), fully map out the solutions, which we analyse in the following.

Starting with
\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}=0
, the two remaining
Eqs. (
104a
)
and (
104b
) decouple and are thus easily solved by any
\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R},\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R}\in\{0,3\epsilon/(n+8)\}
, in line with
Eq. (
105
)
:

\left(\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R},\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R},\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}\right)=\left\{(0,0,0);\penalty\ \left(\frac{3\epsilon}{n+8},0,0\right);\penalty\ \left(0,\frac{3\epsilon}{n+8},0\right);\penalty\ \left(\frac{3\epsilon}{n+8},\frac{3\epsilon}{n+8},0\right)\right\}

(106)

Using these solutions in the stability matrix in
Eq. (
60
)
, we find that, for
\epsilon>0
, it is positive definite (all eigenvalues are positive) only in the last case in
Eq. (
106
)
and only when
n>4
, with eigenvalues
\epsilon
,
\epsilon
and
\epsilon(n-4)/(n+8)
. This gives the first stable fixed point quoted in
Eq. (
61
)
.

We now analyse the case where
\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}\neq 0
, considering first the case
\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}+\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R}=3\epsilon/(n+8)
. In this case,
\beta_{\tilde{g}_{12}}/\tilde{g}_{12}=0
can easily be solved to yield

\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}=\frac{3}{2(n+8)}\frac{w+1}{w+\sigma}\ .

(107)

Using this expression in
\beta_{\tilde{u}_{1}}=0=\beta_{\tilde{u}_{2}}
and solving the quadratic for
\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}
and
\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R}
yields

(\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R},\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R})=\left(\left[1\pm\sqrt{1-\sigma\frac{9n}{n+8}\left(\frac{w+1}{w+\sigma}\right)^{2}}\right]\frac{3\epsilon}{2(n+8)},\left[1\mp\sqrt{1-\sigma\frac{9n}{n+8}\left(\frac{w+1}{w+\sigma}\right)^{2}}\right]\frac{3\epsilon}{2(n+8)}\right)\ ,

(108)

to be read as
two
solutions, as they need to fulfil the constraint
\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}+\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R}=3\epsilon/(n+8)
. However, it turns out that these solutions
Eqs. (
107
)
with (
108
), are
always
unstable, as we discuss in the following. The determinant of the stability matrix
Eq. (
60
)
is

\det(\Lambda)=-\frac{2\epsilon}{3(n+8)}(2(n+8)\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}-3\epsilon)^{2},

(109)

which is negative, implying instability, unless
\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}=\frac{3\epsilon}{2(n+8)}
, in which case the determinant vanishes. For this edge case,
\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}=\frac{3\epsilon}{2(n+8)}
, the bare parameters
\sigma
and
w
have to be fine-tuned such that in
Eq. (
108
)
, the argument of the square root needs to vanish.

However, in this edge case, one eigenvalue of the stability matrix
Eq. (
60
)
still turns out to be negative. In summary,
\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}\neq 0
with
\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}+\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R}=3\epsilon/(n+8)
produces no stable fixed point.

We finally analyse the case
\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}\neq 0
and
\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}=\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R}=\tilde{u}^{*}
. Using
\beta_{\tilde{g}_{12}}=0
to express
\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}
in terms of
\tilde{u}^{*}
,

\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}=\frac{w+1}{4(w+\sigma)}\left(\epsilon-\frac{2(n+2)}{3}\tilde{u}^{*}\right),

(110)

and using this to determine the eigenvalues of the stability matrix, we find that any stable solution needs to satisfy

\tilde{u}^{*}>\frac{\epsilon}{4}\ ,

(111)

with an additional constraint of
\tilde{u}^{*}>3\epsilon/(2(n+8))
which is satisfied for any
n\geq 1
. Using
Eq. (
110
)
to express
\beta_{\tilde{u}_{1}}
solely in terms of
\tilde{u}^{*}
, we can find the explicit solution for
\tilde{u}^{*}
as the root of
\beta_{\tilde{u}_{1}}
,

\tilde{u}^{*}=\frac{3\epsilon}{2(n+8)}\frac{1+\sigma\frac{n(n+2)(w+1)^{2}}{4(w+\sigma)^{2}}\pm\sqrt{1-\sigma\frac{n(4-n)(w+1)^{2}}{4(w+\sigma)^{2}}}}{1+\sigma\frac{n(n+2)^{2}(w+1)^{2}}{4(n+8)(w+\sigma)^{2}}}\ .

(112)

Adding and subtracting
\epsilon/4
from
Eq. (
112
)
, after some algebra we can rearrange the expression for
\tilde{u}^{*}
into

\tilde{u}^{*}_{\pm}=\frac{\epsilon}{4}\left(1+\frac{(4-n)h(\sigma,w,n)}{(n+2)h(\sigma,w,n)\pm 6}\right)

(113)

with

h(\sigma,w,n)\vcentcolon=\sqrt{1-\sigma\frac{n(4-n)(w+1)^{2}}{4(w+\sigma)^{2}}}\ .

(114)

Physical fixed points require
h(\sigma,w,n)
to be real, in which case it is understood to take the positive root, as suggested in
Eq. (
112
)
. Using
Eq. (
113
)
in
Eq. (
110
)
, we obtain an explicit expression for

\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}=\tilde{g}^{*}_{\pm}=\frac{4-n}{4(n+2)}\frac{w+1}{w+\sigma}\frac{\epsilon}{\frac{6}{n+2}\pm h(\sigma,w,n)}.

(115)

To determine the stability of the solution, we return to
Eq. (
111
)
, requiring

\frac{(4-n)h(\sigma,w,n)}{(n+2)h(\sigma,w,n)\pm 6}\geq 0\ ,

(116)

given
Eq. (
113
)
. If there is a physical solution,
h(\sigma,w,n)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, then
Eq. (
116
)
is fulfilled for
\tilde{u}^{*}_{+}
and
n<4
. For
n>4
, the fixed point
\tilde{u}^{*}_{+}
is unstable, whereas for
n=4
it is marginal, coinciding with the rightmost fixed point in
Eq. (
106
)
.

As for
\tilde{u}^{*}_{-}
, for
n<4
the denominator in
Eq. (
116
)
needs to be positive, requiring
h(\sigma,w,n)>6/(n+2)
,

0>\sigma^{2}+2\left(w+\frac{n(n+2)^{2}}{8(n+8)}(w+1)^{2}\right)\sigma+w^{2}\ ,

(117)

which in turn implies

0>(\sigma+\sigma_{+})(\sigma+\sigma_{-})

(118)

with

\sigma_{\pm}\vcentcolon=\left(w+\frac{n(n+2)^{2}}{8(n+8)}(w+1)^{2}\right)\left(1\pm\sqrt{1-\left(\frac{w}{w+\frac{n(n+2)^{2}}{8(n+8)}(w+1)^{2}}\right)^{2}}\right)\ .

(119)

Given that
w
is non-negative, both
\sigma_{+}
and
\sigma_{-}
are bound to be non-negative and real, so that
\sigma\in(-\sigma_{+},-\sigma_{-})
for
Eq. (
118
)
to hold for
n<4
. Again, for
n=4
, the fixed point value
\tilde{u}^{*}_{-}
in
Eq. (
113
)
reduces to the right-most fixed point in
Eq. (
106
)
. Finally, when
n>4
, we require
h(\sigma,w,n)<6/(n+2)
in
Eq. (
116
)
. The ensuing analysis in fact reproduces condition
Eq. (
118
)
with
Eq. (
119
)
, with the factor
(n-4)
in the definition of
h(\sigma,w,n)
,
Eq. (
114
)
, cancelling as
1-6/(n+2)=(n-4)/(n+2)
.

To conclude this case, the fixed point
\tilde{u}^{*}_{-}
we started to analyse with
Eq. (
117
)

(\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R},\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R},\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R})=\left(\frac{\epsilon}{4}\left(1+\frac{(4-n)h(\sigma,w,n)}{(n+2)h(\sigma,w,n)-6}\right),\frac{\epsilon}{4}\left(1+\frac{(4-n)h(\sigma,w,n)}{(n+2)h(\sigma,w,n)-6}\right),\frac{4-n}{4(n+2)}\frac{w+1}{w+\sigma}\frac{\epsilon}{\frac{6}{n+2}-h(\sigma,w,n)}\right)

(120)

is stable for all
n\neq 4
, provided
\sigma\in(-\sigma_{+},-\sigma_{-})
.

In summary, we find the following stable fixed points: Firstly, the right-most of
Eq. (
106
)
,
Eq. (
61
)
in the main text,
\tilde{u}^{*}_{1,R}=\tilde{u}^{*}_{2,R}=3\epsilon/(n+8)
and
\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}=0
, for
n>4
, secondly
Eq. (
113
)
,
Eq. (
61
)
,
u^{*}_{1}=u^{*}_{2}=\tilde{u}^{*}_{+}
and
\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}=\tilde{g}^{*}_{+}
given in
Eq. (
115
)
,
Eq. (
64
)
, for n¡4, and finally
Eq. (
120
)
,
Eq. (
64
)
,
u^{*}_{1}=u^{*}_{2}=\tilde{u}^{*}_{-}
and
\tilde{g}^{*}_{12,R}=\tilde{g}^{*}_{-}
, for
n<4
and
\sigma\in(-\sigma_{+},-\sigma_{-})
.
